ORDERS 

New Delhi, the 21st June 1968 

S-O- 2444 —Whereas the Election Commission is satisfied that Shri Gurdev 
Singh, Village and P. O- Longowal, Tehsil Sangrur, District Sangrur a contesting 
candidate foi election to the House of the People from Sangrur Parliamentary 
constituency, has failed to lodge an account of his election expenses in the manner 
required by the Representation of the People Act, 1951 , and the Rules made 
thereunder; 

And whereas, after considering the representation made by the said candidate, 
the Election Commission is further satisfied that he has no good reason or justifica¬ 
tion for the failure; 

Now, therefore, in pursuance of section 10 A of the said Act, the Election Com¬ 
mission hereby declares the said Shri Gurdev Singh to be disqualified for being 
chosen as, and for being, a member of either House of Parliament or of the 
Legislative Assembly or Legislative Council of a State for a period of three years 
from the date of this order. 

[No. PB-HP/ 12 / 67 ( 5 ) ]

MINISTRY OF COMMERCE 

Nevj Delhi, the 29 th June 1968 

S.O. 2461 .—The following draft rules further to amend the Coir Industry 
Rules, 1954 , which the Central Government proposes to make in exercise of the 
powers conferred by section 26 of the Coir Industry Act, 1959 (45 of 1953 ), are 
hereby published for general Information as required by sub-section (I) of the 
said section and notice is hereby given that the said darft will be taken into 
consideration on or after the 1968 . 

Any objections or suggestions with respect to the said draft received from any 
person before the aforesaid date will be considered by the Central Government. 


Draft Rules 

1. These rules may be called the Coir Industry (Second Amendment) Rules. 

1968 . 

2 . In the Coir Industry Rules. 1954 ,— 

( 1 ) in rule 15 — 

(i) for sub-rules ( 2 ) and ( 3 ), the following sub-rules Shall be substituted;, 
namely:— 

■'( 2 ) The Board may delegate to the Chairman, or the Secretary, or in 
the case of purchases of raw materials for a powerloom factory to 
the Weaving Master of the powerloom factory, such power for enter¬ 
ing into contracts on its behalf as it may think fit, 

by the Chairman, or Vice-Chairman, and by the Secretary, or by 

( 3 ) Contracts shall not be binding on the Board unless they are executed 
the Weaving Master of the powerloom factory, with the previous 
approval of the appropriate authority concerned and the common 
seal of the Board, where necessary, is affixed thereto: 

Provided that the Weaving Master of the powerloom factory and the ■

exceeding three years which Involve expenditure not Exceeding

3316 "THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JUDY 13, J968/ASADHA 22 . 1890 [Daw H— 

MINISTRY OF FOOD, AGRICULTURE, COMMUNITY DEVELOPMENT AND* 

COOPERATION 

(Department of Agriculture) 

(Committee fat ,.the Purpose of Controlling and Supervising Experiments mm 

Animals) 

Bombay, the 7th June 1988 

S.O. 2479.—The following draft of certain, rules which the Committee for cont¬ 
rolling and Supervising Experiments on Animals proposed to make in exercise 
of the powers conferred by section 17 of the Prevention of Cruelty to Animals 
Act, I960 (59 of 1960), is published for general information and notice is hereby 
given that the said draft will be taken into consideration on or after the 15th 
August, 1968. 

Any objections or suggestions which may be received from any person in res¬ 
pect of, the, said draft before the aforesaid date will be considered by the Com¬ 
mittee for- .Ccmtrolling and Supervising Experiments, on Animals. 

EXPERIMENTS ON ANIMALS (CONTROL AND SUPERVISION) RULES, IDS# 

1. Short title.—These Rules may be called the Experiments on Anhniilr 
(Cbntrdl and Supervision) Rules, 1968. 

2. Definitions.—In these Rules, unless the context otherwise requires,— 

(a) “Act” .means the Prevention of Cruelty to Animals Act I960 (69 of 

1960); 

(b) “Committee" means the Committee constituted under section 15 of 

the Act, for controlling and supervising Experiments on Animals; 

(c) “Experiment" means any experiment performed on any living animal 

including an operation for the purpose of discovering something un¬ 
known ,or testing some (hypothesis or establishing or illustrating 
some truth; 

37 Stocking of Animals.—The animals shall. be stocked in following maimer 

namely.- - 

(a) . Animal houses shall be located in a quiet atmosphere undisturbed 

by the traffic, and the premises kept tidy, and hygienic and the Smd- 
tnals protected from draught and extremes of weather; 

(b) Animal cages for small animals and stables for larger animals shall 

be such that animals can live in comfort and overcrowding is avoided; 

(c) Where standards have been laid down by the Indian Standards Insti¬ 

tution, the cages and the stables, as the case may he, shall conform 
to -those standards; 

(d) Animal, attendants shall be suitably trained and sufficiently experienced 

in the duties allotted to them; 

(e) .Animals shall be looked after, before, during and after the experiment* 

by a trained and experienced attendant; 

(f) There shall, be. satisfactory arrangements for looklhg after the animal* 

during off hours and on holidays; 

(2) Nothing in sub-rule (1) shall apply to animals for field experiments per¬ 
formed under emergency or special circumstances. 

4. Conduct of Experiments.-—In conducting experiments on Animals, regard 
shall be had to the following conditions, namely:— 

(a) - Experiments shall be performed In every case by or under the supervi¬ 

sion of persons duly qualified in that "behalf viz., Degree or Diploma 
holders in Medical or Veterinary or other Science or Pharmacy or 
Natural Science of a recognised University or an Institution recog¬ 
nised by Government for the purpose, in a Laboratory adequately 
equipped and staffed for the purpose and under the responsibility of 
the person performing the experiment, In the case of a teaching re¬ 
search or a Pharmaceutical concern, the responsibility therefore shall 
be placed also on the person incharge of the Institution. 

(b) Experiments shall be performed with due care and humanity:. 



Hmc. 3{u)J THE CAZETTE l OF INDIA . JULY 18, 1968/ASADHA 22, LB','0 


331.7 


(c) Minimum number of animals shall -be used in an experiment, but at 

the same time meeting the statistical requirements; 

(d) Experiments involving operative procedure more severe than simple 

inoculation or superficial venesection shall be performed under the 
influence of anaesthetic of sufficient power to prevent the animal 
feeling pain and it shall remain so throughout the experiment, 'Pro¬ 
vided that where the, services: of a Veterinary Surgeon are not avail¬ 
able, a person adequately trained in methods of anaesthesia, shall be 
allowed to administer anaesthesia and the anaesthetist shall remain 
present near the animal till the completion of the experiment; 

(e) Animals which in the course of experiments under the influence of 

anaesthetic are so injured that their recovery would involve pain 
or suffering shall be destroyed humanely while still insensible; 

<f) When there is reason to believe that an animal is suffering abnormal or 
Severe pain at any stage of a continuing experiment, it shall be pain¬ 
lessly destroyed at that; stage without proceeding with the experi¬ 
ment. 

<g) The experiment shall not be performed for the purpose of attaining or 
retaining manual skill. The condition shall not apply to (t) 
students at the Veterinary Colleges, who shall be permitted to per¬ 
form surgical operations on living animals subject to that animals 
are under deep anaesthesia and the operations are performed under 
the direct supervision of an expert teacher (il) experts in the Medical 
field for performing high type of surgery on animals. 

!h) Experiments shall not be performed by way of am Illustration of lecture 
in medical, veterinary and other science schools or colleges and 
hospitals or elsewhere if other teaching devices such as books, models, 
films and the like where available can equally serve the purposes 
provided that wherever possible, such alternative methods like tissue 
culture or Bio-chemical methods may he,used. 

(i) experiments shall not be performed as a public demonstration except 
for advancement of knowledge, 

<j) the substance known as Urari or Gurari or any such paralysant shall 
not be used or administered for the .purpose of any experiment except 
in conjunction with anasesthtic of sufficient depth to produce loss of 
conclousness; 

<k> except for purposes of research, no experiment the result, of which 
is already conclusively known, shall he repeated solely for the pur¬ 
pose of repetition, 

(1) there shall not be applied to the eye of an animal by way of experiment 
.any chemical substance for the purpose of absorption through the 
conjunctival membrane or through the cornea calculated to give pain; 

<m) dogs held for experimental purposes in animal houses shall not be 
debarked.

ment is performed in an institution, the person in charge thereof, shall maintain 
A chronological record of the experiments carried on by him or in the institution 
aa tile case may be, in which shall be entered in brief, the nature of the experi¬ 
ment, lh animals used, the course and the result thereof, manner of Anal' disposal 
of animals and such other information as the Committee may from time to time 
require.

-which is likely to put any person in trade or business in possession of such in¬ 
formation or knowledge, which upholds any existing or prospective patent rights,

New Delhi, the 1st July 1968 

S.O. 2496—In pursuance of section 17 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 
of 1947), the Central Government hereby publishes the following award: of the- 
Industrial Tribunal, (No, 2), Dhanbad, in the industrial dispute between the emp¬ 
loyers in relation to the Dhori Colliery (Post Office Bermo, District Hazaribagh) 
of Messrs Bokaro Ramgarh Coal Company Limited and their raising contractors 
(Sarangarh Coal Company) of the one part and their workmen of the other’ 
part, which was received by the Central Government on the 26th June, 1868. 

BEFORE THE CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL (NO. 2), 

AT DHANBAD 

Present 

Shri Nandagiri Venkata Rao Presiding Officer. 

Reference No. 40 of 1967 

In the matter of an industrial dispute under Section 10((1) (d) of the Industrial' 
Disputes Act, 1947. 

Parties: 

Employers in relation to the Dhori Colliery (Post Office Bermo, District 
Hazaribagh) of Messrs Bokaro and Ramgarh Limited and their ruls- 
ing-cwm-selling contractors (Sarangarh Coal Company) of the one- 
part 


AND 

Their workmen 


Appearances: 

For the employers : Shri D. Narsingh, Advocate. 

For the workmen: Shri Shankar Bose, Secretary, Colliery Mazdoor Sangh; 
State: Bihar. Industry*! Coal- 

Dhanbad, 18th June, 1968 
AWARD 


The Central Government being of opinion that an industrial dispute exists bet¬ 
ween the employers in relation to the Dhori Colliery (Post Office Bermo, District 
Hazaribagh) of Messrs Bokaro and Ramgarh Limited and their raising-cwp- 
selling contractors (Sarangarh Coal Company) of the one part and their work¬ 
men of the other part, by Its Order No. 8/56/66-LRIII dated 18th May, 1965 and 



;33 24 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JULY 13, 1968/ASADHA 22 , 1890 [Part II- 


corrigendum No. 8/56/65-LRIII dated 16th June, 1965 referred to the Central 
Government Industrial Tribunal Dhanbad under section 10(1) (d) of the Industrial 
Disputes Act, 1947 for adjudication the dispute in respect of the matters specified 
. in the schedule thereto- The schedule is extracted below : 

Schedule 

“Whether the action of the workmen of Dhori colliery and Karo group 
of quarries (2. 3 and 4) in going on strike complaining of alleged 
assault of Shri Ganesh Mahato (Sirdar) by Shri Laxman Singh and 
Shri Ram Kirit Singh (Chuprasis) was justified? If so, to what relief 
are they entitled?” 

2. The Central Government Industrial Tribunal, Dhanbad registered the refer¬ 
ence as reference No- 82 of 1965 on its file- Workmen as well as the employers 
filed their statement of demands. While it was pending before the Central 
Government Industrial Tribunal, Dhanbad the proceeding; was transferred to 
this Tribunal, by the Central Government by Its Order No. 8/25/67-LRII dated 
8th May, 1967 under Section 33B of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947. Conse¬ 
quently, the reference is renumbered on the file of this Tribunal as reference 
No. 40 of 1967. 

3. Admittedly, the workmen of Dhori colliery and Karo group of quarries (2, 
3 and 4) (hereinafter referred to as the colliery) went on strike with effect from 
6th April, 1965 and In pursuance of the order of the Government Issued under Sub¬ 
section (3) of Section 10 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 dated 18 th May, 
1965, the strike was called off on 20th May, 1965- The case of the workmen is 
that the strike was the result of a provocation to the workmen, in that the 
‘Manager abused and Insulted and Manager’s chaprasis beat and removed Shri 
Ganesh Mahato (Sirdar), The employers pleaded mat the strike was illegal and 
against the provisions of Sections 22, 23 and 24 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 
1947. They have also raised an objection against sustainability of the reference. 
The workmen were represented by Shri Shankar Bose, Secretary, Colliery Maz- 
door Sangh and the employers by Shri D- Narslngh, Advocate- By consent of 
the employers Exts. W1 and W2 were marked for the workmen and by consent of 
the workmen Exts. Ml and M2 were marked for the employers, No party exa¬ 
mined any witness. 

4. The workmen themselves have admitted In para 8 of their statement of 
demands that there were no conciliation proceedings and that the Government 
took action on its own accord in the interest of Industrial peace and harmoney and 
production. There Is no material that If the workmen had made any demand on 
tire employers in respect of the matter under reference. The reference says, 
"whereas the Central Government is of opinion that an industrial dispute exists 
between the parties.” The word "exists” is of importance to note. The argu¬ 
ment of the learned Advocate for the employers is that there was no “industrial 
dispute” at all about which the Government opined that It existed between the 
parties- The term "industrial dispute” is defined In Section 2(k) of the Indus¬ 
trial Disputes Act, 1947 as meaning any dispute or difference between employers 
•and employers or between employers and workmen, or between workmen and 
workmen which is connected with the employment or non-employment or the 
terms of employment or with the conditions of labour, of any person”. The 
Supreme Court in Sindhu Resettlement Corporation Ltd, Vs. Industrial Tribunal, 
Gujarat and others (1967-68) XXXIII F.J.R. 332) has pointed out that a mere 
demand to the appropriate Government, without a dispute being raised by the 
workmen with their employer, cannot become an industrial dispute. It j-s further 
explained that if no dispute at all was raised by the workmen with the employer, 
any request sent by them to the appropriate Government would only be a 
demand by them and not an industrial dispute between them and their employer. 
In the case before the Supreme Court the workmen had confined thefr deemand to 
the employers to retrenchment compensation and did not make any demand 
for re-instatement. The Court held that any reference for adjudication of the 
question of reinstatement made by the appropriate Government at the request 
of the workmen would not be competent. In this view I find considerable force 
in the argument addressed on behalf of the employers. 

B. As per the reference the workmen of the colliery went on strike. It- Is 
not open to any party to dispute and the Tribunal to” enquire if the workmen 
went on strike or not. in view of the decision of the Supreme Court In Delhi 
Cloth and General Mills Co., Ltd., and their workmen and others (1967-1-L.L.J 
423), In exercise of the powers conferred by the proviso to Sub-clause (vi) of 



3325 


Sec. 3(ii)] THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JULY IS, 1968/ASADHA 22. 1890 


clause (n) of Section 2 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947, the Central Govern¬ 
ment declared the coal industry to be a public utility service for the purpose of 
the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 for the period of 6 months from 8th January, 1985. 
It follows that from 8th January, 1965 to 8th July, 1965 the coal industry was a 
public utility service within the meaning of Section 22 of the Industrial Dispute* 
Act, 1947- Under Clauses (a) and (b) of sub-section (1) of Section 22 of the 
Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 no person employed in a public: utility service shall go 
on strike in breach of contract without giving to the employer notice of strike 
within 6 weeks before striking or within 14 days of giving such notice- In the 
instant case the workmen did not give to the employers any notice contemplated 
under the above provisions of law and went on strike contravening them during 
the prohibited period. Consequently, the strike Is illegal. In India General 
Navigation Railway Co. Ltd- and another and their workmen (1960-1-L.L.J. 13) the 
Supreme Court has held that a strike in respect of a public utility service, which 
is dearly Illegal, could not at the same time be characterised “as perfectly justified”. 
The Court observed “these two conclusions could not in law co-exist. The law has 
made a distinction between, a strike which is Illegal and one which is not but it has 
not made any distinction between an Illegal strike which may be said to be 
justifiable and one which is not justifiable. This distinction Is not warranted by 
the Act and Is wholly misconceived specially in the case of employees In public 
utility service.” 

6. As a result of my above discussions ignoring the legal objection to the sus¬ 
tainability of the reference, 1 hold that the action of the workmen of the col'iery 
(Dhori colliery and Karo group of quarries) (2, 3 and 4) in going on strike comp¬ 
laining of alleged assault of Shri Ganesh Mahato (Sirdar) by Shri Laiman Singh 
and Shri Ram Kirit Singh (Chaprasis) was not justified, and, consequently, they 
are not entitled to any relief, The Award is made accordingly and submitted 
under Section 15 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947. 

(Sd.) N- Venkata Rao,. 

Presiding Officer, 
Central Govt. Industrial Tribunal, 
(No, 2) at Dhanbad.

New Delhi, the 3rd July 1968 

S-O. 2497.—In pursuance of section 17 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 
■of 1947J, the Central Government hereby publishes the following award of the 
Industrial Tribunal, DhanBad, in the Industrial dispute between the employers 
lr, relation to the Jamadoba Colliery of Messrs Tata Iron and Steel Company 
Limited, Jamadoba, Post Office Jealgora, District Dhanbad and their workmen, 
■which was received by the Central Government on the 25th June, 1968- 

BEFORE THE CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL-CUM- 
LABOUR COURT AT DHANBAD 

In the matter of a reference under section 10(1) (d) of the Industrial Disputes 
Act, 1947. 


Reference No. 5 of 1964 

Parties ; 

Employers In relation to the Jamadoba Colliery of M/s. Tata Iron & Steel Co- 
Ltd.. Jamadoba, P. O- Jealgora, District Dhanbad- 

Vs. 

Their Workmen 


Present : 

Shri Kamla Sahal, Presiding Officer. 

Appearances : 

For the Employers ; Shri S. S. Mukherjee, Advocate. 

For the Workmen: Shri D, Narsingh, Advocate. 

Camp: Patna, dated the 16th June, 1968 

AWARD 

The Central Government in the Ministry of Labour, Employment and Rehabili¬ 
tation has, by its order No- 2/72/63-LRII dated the 15th January, 1964, referred 
the dispute as disclosed in the following schedule for adjudication to this Tribunal: 

The Schedule 

1. Whether the dismissal of Shri Balaram, Haulage Khalasi, Ticket No. 

26593, of Jamadoba Colliery by the management of the said Colliery 
with effect from the 18th January, 1962 was justified? 

2. If not, to what relief Is the workman entitled? 

(2) This is a batch of eight cases in which Shri S. S. Mukherjee, Advocate, 
appearing on behalf of the employers took up the preliminary objection that the 
reference was invalid because, at the time of his dismissal, the workman concern¬ 
ed was not a member of a union nor was his case sponsored by a substantial number 
of workmen- This objection prevailed and Shri Raj Kishore Prasad, the then 
Presiding Officer of this Tribunal, rejected all the references- The workmen 
■filed Writ applications in the Patna High Court against the Tribunal’s awards In 
all the eight cases. Their Lordships first took up C- W. J. C. 192 of 1966 which 
arosb out of reference No. 97 of 1963. They laid down the principles that an indivi¬ 
dual dispute turns into an industrial dispute if, before the date of the reference 
a union comes into existence and the workman concerned becomes a member of 
that union or if his case is sponsored by a substantial number of workmen before 
the date of the reference- This is reported in 1967 (II) L.L.J, 663 as between 
Workmen of Jamadoba Colliery of Tata Iron and Steel Company, Ltd., 
and Jamadoba, Colliery of Tata Iron and Steel Company, Ltd., Jamadoba, 
and another. The Writ application arising out of the present reference was num¬ 
bered as C.W.J. C- No, 274 of 1966, Their Lordships took up this case and ref- 
■erring to their decision in C.W.J. C. No, 192 of 1966, set aside the decision of the 
Tribunal in this case also and remanded the case for hearing on merits and 
disposal in accordance with law- I have heard the parties after the remand- 
Shri S. S- Mukherjee attempted to take the preliminary objection that the reference 
was Invalid because the dispute was an Individual and not an industrial dispute 
in view of the fact that the workman concerned did not become a member of 
any union before the date of the reference- I did not permit him to raise this 
objection because Their Lordships have themselves applied the principle of law 
(aid down by them to the facts of this case and have quashed the decision of 



3328 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JULY 13, 1968/ASADHA 22, 1890 [Pakt li¬ 


the Tribunal obviously on the basis that the present reference Is not invalid. 
They have only directed the Tribunal at this stage to decide the case on merits. 

(3) It appears that the permanent haulage khalasi was a man named Gulabi. 
He was on leave. One Md- Sakur did the work of Gulabi overtime on the 12th 
and 13th December, 1959. On the 14th December, Md- Sakur refused to do 
overtime work for the purpose of acting as haulage engine khalasi. The Time- 
Keeper then asked Shri Balaram, the alleged delinquent, whether he would work 
overtime as haulage engine khalasi. The management’s case is that Balaram 
readily agreed to do the work but Balaram’s case is that he was unwilling to do 
the work but he was forcibly assigned the duty. While Balaram was doing the 
Y’Ork, (i) the haulage rope snapped; (it) six loaded mine cars ran down and- 
were badly damaged; (lil) due to terrific shock the motor and cover were borken 
and (iv) Stator and rotar coils were damaged. 

(4) Chargesheet No- 1033/61 dated 18/19th December, 1961 Ext. M- was issued, 
to Balaram and his reply dated the 25th December, 1961 is Ext. Ml. In this 
reply, he has stated that he was not fully conversant with the defect of the haul¬ 
age, that he was sent to work overtime though he expressed his unwillingness to 
go to work, the circumstances under which the damage was caused were beyond 
his control, that all of a sudden the main switch went off which resulted in failure 
of electricity, that the brake was defective and did not work and that it was 
wrong to say that the incident took place due to his negllence in energising the- 
motor in a reverse direction. 


(5) Shri S. N- Pandey, Welfare Officer, was appointed to hold the enquiry whichi 
was fixed for the 27th December 1961. 


(6) The enquiry proceedings are Exts. M4, M5 and M6. The first act which 

Shri Pandey did was to examine Shri Balaram. After recording his statement, he- 
recorded the statement of Shri S. K. Acharior, Time Keeper. Balaram was 

asked to cross examine this witness and he put one or two questions to him. 

The next witness to be examined was Shri D. K. Sen, the Colliery Engineer- Re¬ 
stated that, while lowering six loaded mine cars by 125 H.P. Haulage, Shri Balaram 
had definitely put a reverse current Instead of putting forward current as a 
result of which damage was caused to the motor, mine cars and the rope. At. 
this stage, curiously enough, Shri Sen was asked to cross examine Shri Balaram 
At first, Shri Balaram co-operated and gave his answers'to the questions put by 
Shri Sen. Subsequently, the nature of Shri Sen’s questions became rather techni¬ 
cal and it seems that Shri Balaram felt completely out of his deuth. He started' 
saying in answer to questions put by Shri Sen that he did not know. Ultimately, 
he said that he could not reply to such questions and. so saying he walked out 
of the place- The statement of Md- Sakur was then recorded and. according to 
him, Balaram willingly went to do the work of haulage engine khalasi- The 
Enquiry Officer has written a note under the statement of Shri Md. Sakur that he 1 
was not cross-examined because Shri Balaram had refused to participate in the 
enquiry any further. In his enquiry report (Ext. M7) Shri Pandey has stated 1 
“in the beginning the accused replied to the questions put by the colliery engineer 
hut when certain vital questions connected with the probable cause of the damage 
were asked he refused to reply and walked out”. From the statement that Balaram 
walked out at that stage, it seems that he was not even present at the time when- 
the statement of Md. Sakur was recorded. In any case, the enquiry offleer: 
found that Balaram’s guilt had been established. By a note (Ext. MS) the Mana¬ 
ger recommended Balaram’s dismissal. 

(7) By the letter (Ext. M9) dated the 13th January, 1962 the Chief Mining En¬ 
gineer dismissed Balaram with effect from the 18th January, 1962- 

(8) In the case of Meenglas Tea Estate Vs. Its Workmen reported in 1962" 
(II) L-L.J- 392, Hidayatulla J has laid down the requirements of a good enquiry 
as follows; 

“No witness was examined In support of the charge before the workman- 
was questioned- It Is an elementary principle that a person who- 
is required to answer a charge must know not only the accusation but 
also the testimony by which the accusation is supported, He must 
be given a fair chance to hear the evidence in support of the charge 



Sec. $(ii)] THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JULY 13. 1968/ASADHA 22 , 1*90 


and to put such relevant questions by way of cross-examination as 
he desires. Then he must be given a chance to rebut the evidence 
led against him. This is the barest requirement of an enquiry of 
this character and this requirement must be substantially fulflled be¬ 
fore the result of the enquiry can be accepted- A departure from 
this requirement in effect throws the burden upon the person charged 
to repel the charge without first making it out against him”. 


(8; Keeping in view the above observations, it is clear that Rules of natural 
Justice have been seriously infringed in this case- In the first place, Balaram was 
the first to be examined in this case- At that time, all that he knew was what 
was alleged in the chargesheet issued against him. He does not appear to have 
had any idea of the testimony which would be led against him. 


(9) In the second place, it Is extraordinary that Shri D- K- Sen, a technical 
man, should have been allowed by the Enquiry Officer to cross-examine Balaram in 
order to get his own opinion as to the cause of the incident supported. His cross- 
examination was so Intensive that the alleged delinquent was literally put to 
flight. He must have felt bewildered and lost confidence in the justice of the 
enquiry. 

(10) The Enquiry Officer should have stopped the proceeding when Balaram 
went away from there because it was not of his own accord that Balaram left the 
place. He only left the place when attempt was made to put him in a corner 
while getting him cross-examined on technical matters by an Engineer. The third 
infirmity in the proceedings, therefore, is that the enquiry officer recorded the state¬ 
ment of Md. Sakur in Balaram’s absence Immediately after he went away so that 
he had no opportunity to cross-examine Sakur- 

(11) The fourth infirmity in the proceeding is that Balaram was not asked 
whether he would examine defence witnesses and no opportunity was given to him 
for any such purpose. 

(12) In view of the weaknesses mentioned above, it is clear that the enquiry 
has not been held In accordance with the principles of natural justice. The alleged 
delinquent was deprived of his right of defending himself and his cross-examination 
by Shri D. K. Sen was extremely prejudicial to him. Hence, the finding arrived at 
by the Enquiry Officer cannot be upheld, Shri D. K. Sen wag examined before 
me after the case was remanded- In his evidence, he has supported his opinion 
that the incident took place because the Haulage Khalasi applied the reverse cur¬ 
rent to the motor suddenly. No evidence was adduced before the Tribunal to 
the effect that Balaram held himself out to be competent to drive 125 H. p. Haul¬ 
age or that he went willingly to act as haulage engine khalasi. If the evidence 
adduced at the enquiry is ignored—and it must be ignored on account of the 
evidence being recorded against the principles of natural justice—no definite find¬ 
ing can be arrived at on the allegations made in the chargesheet against Balaram 
on the basis of the evidence adduced before the Tribunal. 


(13) In the circumstances mentioned above, I hold that the dismissal of Shri 
Balaram Is not justified. That being so, he is entitled to the relief that he should 
be reinstated with effect from the date of his dismissal with all his back wages aa 
well as continuity of service- This is my award. Let it be submitted to the 
Central Government under section 15 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947- 

(Sd.) Kamla Sahai, 

Presiding Officer. 

[No- 2/72/83-LRII] 


S.O. 2498.—In pursuance of section 17 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14

Industrial Tribunal, Dhanbad, in the industrial dispute between the employers in 
relation to the Sijua Colliery of Messrs Tata Iron and Steel Company Limited, 
Jamadoba, Post Office Jealgora, District Dhanbad and their workmen, which was 
received by the Central Government on the 25th June, 1988. 



3330 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JULY 13, 1968/ASADHA 22, 1890 [Part II- 


BEFORE THE CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL-CUM- 
LABOUR COURT AT DHANBAD. 

In the matter of a reference under section 10(1) (d) of the Industrial Dis¬ 
putes Act, 1947, 

Reference No. 9 of 1964 

Parties: 

Employers In relation to the Sijua Colliery of Messrs Tata Iron and Steel 
Co., Ltd., Jamadoba, P.O, Jealgora, (Dhanbad). 

Versus 

Their Workmen. 

Present: 

Shri Kamla Sahai .—Presiding Officer. 

Appearances: 

For the Employers .—Shri S. S. Mukherjee, Advocate. 

For the Workmen .—-Shri D. Narsingh, Advocate. 

Cmmp: Patna, dated the 18th June, 1968. 

AWARD 

This is one of the batch of eight references which has been remanded by the 
Patna High Court to this Tribunal, By Its order No. 2/2/64-LRU dated the 22nd 
January, 1964, the Central Government made this reference to this Tribunal for 
adjudication of the dispute mentioned in the schedule which is as follows: — 

Schedule 

“Whether the action of the management of Messrs Tata Iron and Steel Com¬ 
pany, Jamadoba, Post Office, Jealgora, in dismissing Shri Ramautar 
Coalfield Recruiting Organisation Miner from service in Sijua Col¬ 
liery, was justified? If not, to what relief is the employee entitled?” 

2. Shri Raj Kishore Prasad, who was then the Presiding 1 Officer of the Tribunal 
took up all the references, two of which were this reference and reference No. 9 1 } 
of 1963. In all the eight cases, Shri S. S, Mukherjee, Advocate, appearing for 
the employers, took the preliminary objection that these were cases of individual 
disputes and not industrial disputes because the workmen concerned were 
not members of any union nor were their cases sponsored by a substantial 
number of workmen before their dismissal. Shri Raj Kishore Prasad 
upheld this objection and rejected all the eight references. The workmen 
filed Writ applications in the Patna High Court against his decisions. C.W.J.C, 
192 of 1966 arose out of reference No. 97 of 1963 and C.W.J.C. 275 of 1966 arose 
out of the present reference. Their Lordships gave a reasoned judgement in 
C.W.J.C. 192 of 1966 and set aside the decision of this Tribunal on the ground 
that an Individual dispute turns into an industrial dispute if, before the reference, 
the union comes into existence and the concerned workman join the union or if 
a substantial number of workmen sponsors his cause. This decision has been 
reported as Workmen of Jamadoba Colliery of Tata Iron and Steel Company, 
Ltd., and Jamadoba Colliery of Tata Iron and Steel Company, Ltd., Jamadoba, 
and another [1967 (II) L.L.J. 663]. When their Lordships took up C.W.J.C. 275 
of 1966, they referred to the decision given by them in C/W.J.C. 192 of 1966. They 
also mentioned that Mr, S. B. Sanyal had urged before them that the finding of 
the Tribunal that the workman concerned became a member of the union was 
based on no evidence. In connection withlthis argument, their Lordships have said 
that the Trbunal had discussed the materials and held that the workman became 
a member of the union after the date of his dismissal and that this was a finding 
of fact which could not be agitated In the High Court. Following the decision in 
C.W.J.C. 192 of 1966, Their Lordships set aside the award of this Tribunal and 
remanded the case, directing that the reference should be re-heard on merits and 
it should be disposed of in accordance -with law. 

3, Shri S. S. Mukherjee has attempted again to raise the preliminary objection 
but the High Court has not only laid down the principle of law in C.W.J.C. 192 
Of 1966 but it has applied the principle specifically to tills case also on its own 
facts. That being so, I -did not permit Shri Mukherjee to agitate the preliminary 
objection again, 



3331 


Sec. 3(11)] THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JULY IS, 1968/ASADHA 22. 1690 


4. The allegation against Ramautar, Miner, C.R.O, (Coalfield Recruiting Orga-. 
nlsation) is that, at about 0-30 a.m. on 4th October, 1963, he assaulted Shn Bashir 
Md. C. R. O. Miner while on duty at 12 Pit landing Golai, in the presence of other 
co-workers. The management issued ehargesheet No. 462 (Ext. M) dated the 
12/14th October. 1963, to Shri Ramautar on this aPegation. Ramautar submitted 
his reply (Ext. Ml) on the 15th October, 1963. In the reply, he alleged that he 
was falsely implicated on false allegation because the Hostel Superintendent and 
the Welfare Officer were annoyed with him on account of his activities in enrol¬ 
ling members of the Congress Mnzdoor Sangh and in holding meetings of the 
C.R.O. workers for not taking meal in the common kitchen. 

5. Shri R, K. Varma, Welfare Officer, was appointed to be the Enquiry Officer. 
He issued a notice (Ext. M2) on the 23rd October, 1963 to Ramautar, stating that 
he had previously been informed that the enquiry would be held on the 23rd 
October. 1963, but he bad not cared to come on that day and that he was once 
again being advised to attend the enquiry which was now to be held on the 25th 
October, 1963. 

6. On the 25th October, 1963, the Enquiry Officer recorded the statement of 
Shri Laloo Singh (Ext. M3), Ramdeo (Ext. M4) and Ramadhar (Ext, M5). All 
the three witnesses stated that Ramautar came to the enquiry but told the Wel¬ 
fare Officer that he would not make any statement or attend, the enquiry and 
that he then went away and did not take any further part in the enquiry j It 
appears that the enquiry officer then recorded the evidence of Shri M. S. Hazra, 
Bashir Mohammad, Ramadhar Kewat, Ramdeo Ahir and Sohanlal (Exts M6 to 
M10) who supported the alleged incident of assault by Ramautar on Bashir 
Mohammad either as a hear-say or as an eye-witness. 

7. In the report (Ext. Mil) dated 25th October, 1963, Shri R. K. Varma, the 
Enquiry Officer, has mentioned that Ramautar was asked to attend the enquiry 
on 23rd October, 1963, but he did not turn. It is not clear how this informa¬ 
tion is alleged to have been given to Ramautar. I have already mentioned that, 
by a notice (Ext. M2), Ramautar was asked to appear at the enquiry on 25th 
October, 1963. The Enquiry Officer then added that the accused accordingly re¬ 
ported to him at 9-00 a..m on 25th October, 1963, but he refused to give his 
Statement or to take part in the enquiry. When the enquiry started, he left the 
office saying “that he will not participate In the enquiry”. He has subsequently 
commented that, from the fact that Ramautar did not participate in the enquiry, 
it seemed that he had nothing to say In his defence. On this basis, he held Ram- 
autar on ex-parte evidence to be guilty of the charge. 

8. The Manager’s letter (Ext, M12) dated 30t.h October, 1963, shows that an 
earlier date of enquiry was the 21st October, 1963 but there seems to be no 
material to show how information relating to that date was conveyed to 
Ramautar. The letter further shows that when Ramautar came to attend 
the enquiry on the 25th October, he asked for 10 days time to prepared his 
defence and filed a letter to that effect. The letter was replied to. The Wel¬ 
fare Officer (P) advised Ramautar to attend the enquiry saying that the enquiry 
would otherwise be conducted ex-parte. On the basis of these facts, he recom¬ 
mended dismissal. 

9. Ext. M13 is a letter dated the 4th/5th November, 1963, addressed by the 
Agent and Aastt., Chief Mining Engineer of the Sijua Group of collieries to Shri 
Ramautar. The information conveyed under this letter Is that Ramautar was 
being dismissed from the company's service with effect from 13th November, 1963. 

10. When I took up this case after remand, the employers examined Shri 
R. K. Varma, the Welfare Officer, who held the ehargesheet enquiry in this case. 
Among other thing he has stated that, when Ramautar came to the place of 
enquiry on 25th October, 1963, he requested him (the Welfare Officer, for 10 
days time to produce his defence witnesses and filed an application (Ext, M15) 
for that purpose. The Enquiry Officer says that he told Ramautar that, since 
the managements’ witnesses had come, he would like to examine them and then 
to give time to Ramautar to produce his witnesses and that thereafter Ram¬ 
autar went away without participating in the enquiry. 

11. It must be clear from what I have already stated that a curious feature of 
this case is that, in their statements (Exts, M3 to M5), the first three witnesses 
who were examined on the 25th October, did not say anything about the request 
of Ramautar for time to produce witnesses. In his report (Ext. Mil) also, the 
Enquiry Officer made no mention whatsoever about such a request—much less 
about an application. It is for the first time that the Manager made reference 
to Ramautar’s request and a letter field by him for that purpose In his letter 



3332 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JL'I.Y 13, 1968/ASADHA 22, 1390 [Part II— 


(Jtxt, M12) dated the 30th October, 1963. It is only before this Tribunal for 
the first time that Shri Varma has not only produced the application (Ext. M16) 
but has also stated that he told Ramautar that he would like to record the evi¬ 
dence of the prosecution witnesses and then to give time to him to examine Ilia 
witnesses. I find it extremly difficult to accept such a belated statement made 
about five years after the events took place- I may refer in this connection 
also to Ext. W4 which is a lettr addrased by the Chief Mining Engineer to Sri 
Ramautar. It is dated 20/22nd November, 1963—long after the letter of dis¬ 
missal was issued. Although this letter purports to be the reply to a letter 
dated the 25th October, 1983, it seems to be the reply to some letter other than 
Ext M15. I say so because the only point raised in Ext. M15 is that 10 days’ 
time might be granted. This has not been replied to in Ext- W4- All that has 
been stated therein is that, since Ramautar refused to make any statement to 
the Enquiry Officer and left the office, there was no alternative for the Enquiry 
Officer but to conduct the enquiry ex-•parte. 


12. Prom the circumstances mentioned above, it appears to me that Ram-* 
autar prayed for adjournment of the enquiry for 10 days so that he might be 
enabled to produce his witnesses and filed an aplication with that prayer. 
The Enquiry Officer ignored the prayer and wished to proceed on the footing 
that Ramautar made no request nor any statement. This is clear from the evi¬ 
dence of the first three witnesses as recorded by the Enquiry Officer. Finding, 
however, that the Manager had mentioned in his letter (Ext. M12) that there 
was a request and a letter by Ramautar, he was forced to make that admission 
and to produce the application (Ext. M15) before the Tribunal. He then came 
out with the explanation that he told Ramautar that he would examine the 
management's witnesses and then grant him 10 days time for production of his 
defence witnesses. If the case was being adjourned for 10 days, there was 
absolutely no point in recording the statement of prosecution witnesses on that 
date and then to grant adjourment, Ramautar might have said that his wit¬ 
nesses could give him information and could enable him to cross-examine the 
prosecution witnesses also. On a consideration of these facts, I am not pre¬ 
pared to accept the Enquiry Officer’s statement about what he told Ramautar in 
connection with his prayer for time. 


13. The incident in question is alleged to have taken place on the 4th Octo¬ 
ber, 1963. The management claims to have started fixing dates of enquiry from 
the 21st October. The proceeding cannot be said to have been delayed on the 
26th October, 1963. The letter conveying the order of dismissal is dated the 
4th/5th November, 1963. Surely, the Enquiry Officer could, without any diffi¬ 
culty, grant 10 days adjournment of the enquiry to Ramautar. No material has 
been shown to me which could lead me to the conclusion that Ramautar’s re- 

? [uest for time was un-reasonable or for the purpose of delaying the proceeding, 
t was the first date on which he had attended the enquiry and from, the facta 
that no time was given to him on his request, that there was a denial of op¬ 
portunity to him to put up his defence by cross-examining the management’s 
witnesses and examining his own defence witnesses, it is manifest that the in¬ 
quiry has not been conducted legally and Ihe principles of nautral justice have 
not been kept in view. It is therefore undoubtedly vitiated. I therefore, hold 
that the dismissal of Ramautar is not justified. In my opinion, he is entitled to 
pe reinstated from the date of his dismissal with all his back wages along with 
continuity of service. This is my award and it may now be submitted to the 
Central Government under section 18 of the Act. 

Sd./- Kamla Sahai, Presiding Officer,

BEFORE THE CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL (NO. 2 ) 

AT DHANBAD. 


Present: 

Shrl Nandagiri Venkata Rao.— Presiding Officer. 

Reference No, 77 of 1967 

In the matter of an industrial dispute under Section 16(1) (d) of the Indus¬ 
trial Disputes Act, 1947, 

Parties: 

Employers in relation to the M/s. Kumardhubi Colliery of M/s. K. Worah 
and Company Private Limited, P.O. Chirkunda, District Dhanbad. 


AND 

Their Workmen. 


Appearances: 

Fr the employers .—Shri B. P.Dabral, Chief Personnel Officer. 

For the workmen .—Shri Shankar Bose, Secretary, Collery Mazdoor Sangh 

State: Bihar. Industry: Coal. 


Dhanbad, the 21st June, 1968. 

AWARD 

The Central Government, being of opinion that an industrial dispute exists 
between the employers in relation to the M/s. Kumardhubi Colliery of M/s. K. 
Worah and company Private Limited, P.O. Chirkunda, District Dhanbad and 
their workmen, by its order No. 2/58/65/LRII dated’ 11th October, 1965, referred 
to the Central Government Industrial Tribunal, Dhanbad under Section 16(1) (d) 
of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947, for adjudication the dispute in respect of the 
matters specified in the schedule annexed thereto. The schedule is extracted 
below; 


Schedule 

"Whether the stoppage from work of Sarvashri Janki Bhuia, Jamuna Bhuia 
and Devsaran Thakur, Trammers, with effect from the JWth October, 
1964, and of Jagamath Bhuia, Trammer, with effect from 28th Octo¬ 
ber, 1964, and their subsequent dismissal from service with effect 
from the 23rd November. 1964, by the management of M/s. East 
Kumardhubi Colliery of M/s. K. Worah and company Private Limited, 
P.O. Chirkunda, District Dhanbad was an act of victimisation. If so, 
to what relief are these workmen entitled?” 

2. The Central Government Industrial Tribunal, Dhanbad registered the re¬ 
ference as reference No. 171 of 1965 on its file, Employers as well as workmen 
filed their statements of demands. While it was pending before the Central Gov¬ 
ernment Industrial Tribunal, Dhanbad the proceeding was transferred to this 
Tribunal by the Central Government by its order No. 8/25/67-LRII dated 8th May, 
1967, under Section 33 R of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947. Consequently, the 
reference is renumbered on the file of this Tribunal as reference No. 77 of 1967. 
Employers filed an addendum to their statement of demands and workmen filed 
rejoinder to the statement of demands and addendum filed by the employers. 

3. In the dispute involved in the reference there are 4 workmen who are 
affected, S/Shri Janki Bhuia, Jamuna Bhuia, Devsaran Thakur and Jagamath 
Bhuia. The first three affected workmen were permanent trammers. All the 
4 affected workmen were the workmen of Kumardhubi Colliery (hereinafter re¬ 
ferred to as the colliery). All the 4 affected workmen were served with charge- 
sheets dated 30th October, 1964, and as a result of a jont domestic enquiry their 
services were terminated with effect from 23rd November, 1964. These facts are 
not in dispute. The dispute was raised before the Regional Labour Commissioner 
(Central), Dhanbad on behalf of the workmen by the General Secretary, Colliery 
Mazdoor Sangh. The case of the workmen is that the first 3 affected workmen 
were stopped from work with effect from 30th October, 1904, and the 4th Shri 
Jagamath Bhuia with effect from 28th October, 1004, and ultimately all the 4 
affected workmen were dismissed from service witl effect from 23rd November, 



333n THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JULY 13, 1968/ASADHA 22, 1890 


1964, to victimise them for their trade union activities. According to the work- 
men the suspension of the affected workmen was in contravention of the Standing 
Orders, that the charges brought against them were false and concocted and that 
the domestic enquiry held against them was improper and the findings arrived 
at by the enquiry officer were perverse. The stand taken by the employers is 
that the dispute involved in the reference was not an industrial dispute but it 
was only an individual dispute, that the 4th affected workman, Shri Jagarnath 
Bhuia was only' a badli trammer and he was stopped from work with effect from 
30th October, 1964, and not from 28th October, 1964, that charge-sheets were issu¬ 
ed to all the 4 affected workmen on 30th October, 1964 for committing miscon¬ 
duct by assaulting the Manager of the colliery and stopping the 4 workmen pend¬ 
ing enquiry, that the domestic enquiry was held in presence of all the 4 affected 
workmen, that in the enquiry guilt was brought home to the 4 affected workmen, 
that as a result of the enquiry and as recommended by the enquiry officer the 4 
affected workmen were dismissed from service and that the action of the em¬ 
ployers taken against the 4 affected workmen was proper and just. They have 
categorically denied the allegation of victimisation. The workmen were repre¬ 
sented by Shri Shankar Bose, Secretary, Colliery Mazdoor Sangh and the em¬ 
ployers by Shri B, P, Dabral, Chief Personnel Officer. By consent of the em- 
loyera, Exts. Wl to Wll were marked for the workmen and on admission by 
the workmen, Exts, Ml to M12 were marked for the employers. On behalf of 
the workmen a witness was examined and Exts. W12 and W12A and M13 were 
marked. On behalf of the employers 2 witness were examined and Exts. M14 to 
M50 were marked. 


4. At the outset the employers had raised an objection that the dispute in¬ 
volved In the reference was only an individual dispute and not an industrial dis¬ 
pute. The contention of the employers is that the Branch of the Colliery Mazdoor 
Sangh working at the colliery did not raise the dispute, that a large majority of 
the members of the Executive Committee of the Branch Sangh was opposed to 
raising any industrial dispute in respect of the 4 affected workmen and that the 
Central Colliery Mazdoor Sangh could not convert an individual dispute into an 
industrial dispute. For support they have relied upon the evidence of WW1 and 
documents. The evidence of WW1 is not of much consequence to either of the 
parties, except to prove some documents. WW1 has deposed that, in the year 
1064 he was a Secretary of the Branch Sangh at the colliery and subsequently 
cameforth with the statement that he was elected as an Assistant Secretary in 
the election held in September, 1964. But Ext. M10, which Is a letter addressed 
by Shri R. N, Sharma, M.L.A., General Secretary of the Colliery Mazdoor Sangh 
to the Manager of the colliery dated 8th September, 1964, appending a list of 
newly elected office bearers of the Branch Sangh at the colliery for the year 
1964-65, does not show the name of WW1 as the Assistant Secretary, istead Shri 
Ram Kishun Das is shown elected as the Assistant Secretary- But it is in the 
evidence of WW1 that in the year 1964 there were about 700 or 800 members of 
the Colliery Mazdoor Sangh at the Branch Sangh at the colliery. This fact is 
not rebutted by the employers. Ext, M50 is a report of the meeting of the 
Executive Committee of the Branch Sangh at the colliery stated to have been 
held on the evening on 22nd January, 1965, stating that the meeting was held 
under the Presidentship of Shri R, N. Sharma, M.L.A. and was attended by 37 
members. It further says that a resolution was passed with the concurrance of 
30 members present, that the committee disassociated and itself from Illegal actions 
committed by members by way of violation or strike and wished that whatever 
action proposed to be taken by the President must have the concurrance of the 
Vice President and the Assistant Secretary of the Branch. It is to be remem¬ 
bered that Ext. M10 clearly shows that Shri R, N. Sharma, M.L.A. was not only 
the General Secretary of the Colliery Mazdoor Sangh but he was also the Presi¬ 
dent of the Branch Sangh at the colliery. The failure report of the Regional 
Labour Commissioner (C) Dhanbad dated 14th May, 1965, annexed to the order 
of reference shows that the dispute was raised before him by the General Secre¬ 
tary, Colliery Mazdoor Sangh, P.O. and District Dhanbad by a letter No. VII(3A)/ 
64-65/7640-41 dated the 8th February. 1964. This letter is no other than Ext. W5, 
which is marked on admission by the employers. The letter was addressed by 
Shri R. N. Sharma, M.L.A., General Secretary. The letter was addressed after 
Shri R. N. Sharma, M.L.A. was elected as Ihe President of the Branch Sangh at 
the colliery, as is seen by Ext. M10 and after the meeting of the Executive Com¬ 
mittee dated 22nd January, 1965, was stated to have passed the resolution. Ac¬ 
cording to the resolution whatever action was proposed by the President must 
have had the concurrance of the Vice President and the Assistant Secretary of 
the Branch Sangh, According to Ext., M10, Shri Sadhu Pashi and Shri Ram 



Sec. S(il)] THF. GAZETTE OF INDIA : JULY 13, 1968/ASADHA 22. 1890 


Prawesh Mahato were Vice Presidents and Shri Ram Kishun Das was the Assis¬ 
tant Secretary. Thej-e is no evidence that the letter W6 submitted to the Re¬ 
gional Labour Commissioner(C) Dhanbad on 8th February, 1965, by tlie Presi¬ 
dent, Shri R. N. Sharma, M.L.A. had not the concurrence of the Vice Presidents 
or the Assistan Secretary. Neither Sri R. N. Sharma, M.L.A. the President 
Shri Sadhu Pashi and Shri Ram Prawesh Mahato Vice Presidents. Shri Ram 
Kishun Das, Assistant Secretary nor any of them is examined. Under these 
circumstances it is not correct to say that the dispute before the Regional Labour 
Commissioner(C) Dhanbad was not raised by the Branch Sangh of the Colliery 
Mazdoor Sangh or that it was raised by the Central Union of the Sangh, Conse¬ 
quently, the objection cannot sustain. It is over-ruled. 

5. The charge-sheets issued to the 4 affected workmen are Exts. M14, M15, 
M16 and M17, They are all dated 30th October, 1964. In Ext. M14 the affected 
workman Shri Jagarnath Bhuia is described as a badli trammer. The case of 
the workmen is that Shri Jagarnath Bhuia wus a permanent trammer. But there 
is no material brought on record to show that Shri Jagarnath Bhuia was a per¬ 
manent trammer and not a badli trammer. Exts. M2 and M3 are letters dated 
respectively 12th October, 1964. and 22nd October, 1964, addressed by Shri R. N. 
Sharma, M.L.A., General Secretary, Colliery Mazdoor Sangh to the Manager of 
the colliery complaining that the affected workman, Shri "Jagarnath Bhuia was, 
not being given work six days in a week. In Ext, M3 it was stated that the 
affected workman, Shri Jagarnath Bhuia and some others were told by the 
management that they were temporary while several of the workmen who were 
juniors to the affected workmen -were given permanent jobs. Ext. M38 is a reply 
by the Manager of the colliery to Ext., M3, pointing out therein that the affected 
workman, Shri Jagarnath Bhuia was borne in the list of badli trammers and 
that he was being given badli employment in place of permanent trammers who 
absented themselves on account of leave, sickness, etc. The letter shows that 
even before the charge-sheet, Ext. M14 was issued, the affected workman, Shri 
Jagarnath Bhuia was treated as a badli trammer and not as a pemanent trammer. 
It is contended on behalf of the workmen and it is also stated in the reference 
that the affected workman, Shri Jagarnath Bhuia stopped from work from 
28th October, 1964, while the employers pleaded that there was no question of 
his being stopped from work as he was a badli workman and he was not provid¬ 
ed with work when there was none to provide him. Even the affected workman,. 
Shri Jagarnath Bhuia did not go into the witness box to show that he was a per¬ 
manent trammer. On this material I hold that the affected workman, Shri Jagar¬ 
nath Bhuia was only a badli trammer and as such the employers could not be 
found fault for not providing him with work with effect from 28th October, 1964. 

6. As I have already pointed out, the charge-sheets issued to the 4 affected 
workmen are Exts. M14, M15, M16 and M17. No where in the statement of 
demands was it stated that the charge-sheets were not served on the affected 
workman or that they had no knowledge of the domestic enquiry held against 
them. On the contrary, it was stated in paras 5 and 12 of thir statement of 
demands that all the affected workmen were served with charge-sheets, while 
pleading that they contained false and concocted charges. Shri B. P. Dabral, who 
was the Group Labour Officer in 1964 had conducted the domestic enquiry against 
all the 4 affected workmen and he is examined as MW1. It is in his evidence 
that all the 4 affected workmen were present throughout the enquiry and some of 
them cross examined the management’s witnesses also. He held the joint enquiry 
against all the 4 affected workmen on 19th November 1965, and the enquiry 
proceedings are Ext, M23. Exts. M21 and M22 are explanations to the charge- 
sheets submitted by the 2 affected workmen, Shri Jamuna Bhuia and Shri Janki 
Bhuia. In the enquiry proceedings, Ext. M23 there are statement of all the 4 
workmen recorded by MW1 and the statements are signed by the affected work¬ 
men respectively and they are proved by MW1. The proceedings, Ext. M23 also 
show that the affected workmen examined 3 witnesses in their defence and this 
fact is also proved by MW1. On the face of this record it cannot be argued that 
the affected workmen had no knowledge of the enquiry or charges brought 
against them. Shri Shankar Bose, the learned representative of the workmen 
has referred me to para 7 of the statement of demands filed on behalf of the 
employers and contended that while the domestic enquiry was held on 19th 
November, 1964. 3 of the affected workmen, Shri Devsaran Thnkur, Shri Jamuna 
Bhuia and Shri Jagarnath Bhuia were served with charge-sheets respectively 
on 23rd November, 1964, 17th November, 1964, and 21st November, 1964 and 
as such, the affected workmen had no opportunity to submit their explanations 
or participate in the domestic enquiry. But, as I have already pointed out all 
the 4 affected workmen did participate in the domestic enquiry whether they 
were served with the charge-sheets or not. MW1 deposes that they attended 



333 ® 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JULY 13, 1%6/ASADHA 22, 1890 [Part li¬ 


the enquiry on oral information. There is no reason to infer otherwise. None 
of the affected workmen is examined to show that he did not participate in the 
domestic enquiry ot his statement was not recorded by the enquiry officer or ha 
did not sign the statement. The enquiry proceedings show and MW1 has sworn 
that the affected workmen were given sufficient opportunity to cross examine the 
management’s witnesses and lead their defence. There is no plea that the findings 
arrived at by the enquiry officer were perverse. Having gone through the en¬ 
quiry proceedings, Ext M23 and the statement of the management’s witnesses and 
the defence witnesses, 1 cannot conclude that the findings were perverse. It in 
contended on behalf of the workmen that 3 of the affected workmen were acquit¬ 
ted by the First Class Magistrate on the same charge and on the evidence of the 
same witnesses. The Magistrate had weighed the evidence adduced before him 
and the enquiry officer, MW1, the evidence recorded by him and arrived at their 
findings. Although the same witnesses were examined before the Magistrate and 
the enquiry officer, MW1, it cannot be stated with certainty that the evidence 
tendered by the witness was exactly the same. Further, the enquiry officer is 
not bound to substitute the findings of the Magistrate for his own. It is not 
shown in what manner the findings were unjust or the enquiry was in contraven¬ 
tion of the principles of natural justice. It is also not the case of the workmen 
that there was any violation of any law or Standing Orders in the employer* 
accepting the findings of the enquiry officer and dismissing the affected work¬ 
men. 


7. The case of the workmen was that the dismissal of the affected workmen 
was with a view to victimise them for their trade union activities. But there 1* 
no c linchin g evidence to give rise to this inference. Not even the affected 
workmen are examined. I do not find any substance in the allegation. 


8. As per clause 27 of the Standing Orders of the colliery. Ext W10 no em¬ 
ployee can be suspended without pay, whether as a punishment or pending en¬ 
quiry exceeding for more than 10 days. This position is admitted by the em¬ 
ployers. 


9. As I have pointed out above, the employers cannot be found fault for stop¬ 
ping the affected workmen, Shri Jagamath. Bhuia from work from 28th October, 
1964, as he was a badlt trammer. As regards the other 3 affected workmen the 
charge-sheets were issued on 30th October, 1964, and at the time suspending them 
from their service from the same date. All the 4 affected workmen are dismis¬ 
sed from service with effect from 23rd November, 1964. Thus, the period of sus¬ 
pension of the 3 affected workmen, viz,, Sarvashxi Janki Bhuia, Jumuna Bhuia and 
Devsaran Thakur was for 24 days and exceeding the 10 days limit prescribed in 
clause 27 of the Standing Orders, Ext, W10. The contention of the employers is 
that the suspesion period should be counted from the date on which the charge- 
sheets were served on the concerned affected workmen. But this contention 
cannot be accepted in view of the fact that the employers kept the 3 affected 
workmen under suspension actually from 30th October, 1964 and it is not their 
case that they allowed the 3 affected workmen to continue in their service till the 
date of service of charge-sheets on them and paid them their wages and other 
dues accordingly. In this view the 3 affected workmen will be entitled to their 
wages and other emoluments for 14 days. 


10. I, therefore, hold that the dismissal from service with effect from 23rd 
November, 1984, of Sarvashri Janki Bhuia, Jamuna Bhuia, Devsaran Thakur and 
Jagamath Bhuia by the management of M/s. East Kumardhubi Colliery of M/s. 
K. Worah and Company Private Limited, P.O. Chirkunda, District Dhanbad was 
not an act of victimisation and, it was justified. But, suspension of Sarvashri 
Janki Bhuia, Jumuna Bhuia and' Devsaran Thakur for 14 days was not justified, 
and as such, they are entitled to their wages and other emoluments for the 14 
days. Award is made accordingly and submitted under Section 15 of the Indus¬ 
trial Disputes Act, 1947. 


SdL/- N. Venkata Rao, 
Presiding Officer, 

Central Government Industrial Tribunal 
(No. 2) at Dhanbad.

[No. 2/58/6&-LRIIJ 


New Delhi, the Bth July 1908 

S.O. 2500.—In pursuance of section 17 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 

of 1947), the Central Government hereby publishes the following award of the 
Industrial Tribunal, Dhanbad, in the industrial dispute between the employers in 
relation to the Ballhari Colliery of Messrs Balihari Colliery Company (Private) 
Limited (and their Managing Agents, Messrs Khatau and Company (Private) 
Limited), Post Office Kusunda, District Dhanbad and their workmen, which was 
received by the Central Government on the 2nd July, 1908. 

BEFORE THE CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL CUM LABOUR 

COURT AT DHANBAD 

In the matter of a reference under section 10(l)(d) of the Industrial Dispute* 
Act, 1947. 

Reference No. 58 of 1967 


Parties :

AWARD 

By order No. 2/124/64-LRII dated the 11th Junuary, 1905, the Government ot 
India made this reference to this Tribunal for adjudication and by Its order 
No. 8/23/67-LRII dated the 10th September, 1967, it was transferred to the Central 
Government Industrial Tribunal at Jabalpur. It was numbered there as reference 
No. 132 of 1967. By another order, bearing No. 8/25/67-LRII dated the 24th 
November, 1967 the reference has been re-transferred to this Tribunal- It has now 
been numbered as reference No. 58 of 1967- The dispute which has to be adjudicat¬ 
ed upon is mentioned in the schedule which is as follows: — 

Schedule 

(1) Whether the management of the Balihari Colliery of Messrs Balihari 
Colliery Company (Private) Limited [and their Managing Agents, 
Messrs Khatau and Company (Private) Limited] rendered the following 
workman Idle/or stopped them from their work with effect from the 
dates shown against their names. If so, whether their action was 
justified? If not justified to what relief are the workmen entitled? 


Name of the workmen 

Date from which rendered idle? 

1. Shri Began Ram 


12-8-64 

2. Shri Barhan Bhulya

(2) Whether the Management of Balihari Colliery of Messrs Balihari Colliery 
Company (Private) Limited [and their Managing Agents Messrs 
Khatau and Company (Private) Limited], were justified In stopping 
from work Shri Ram Khelawan Rauth, Pump Khalasj with effect from 
the 27th August, 1964 and keeping Shrl Chulhan Sao, Under Ground 
Trammer, under suspension from the 21st August, 1904 and finally dis¬ 
missing him from service with effect from the 14th September, 1904? 
If not to what relief are the workmen entitled?" 

2- Both parties have entered into a compromise and have filed today a com¬ 
promise petition dated the 22nd June, 1968. Annexure ‘A’ to the petition is a list 
of all the 12 workmen mentioned in both parts of the reference and, against the 
name of each is mentioned the amount of retrenchment compensation which Is 
payable to him. The management has agreed to treat these workmen as having 
been retrenched and, on that basis, the retrenchment compensation is being paid- 
The parties have stated before me through their representatives that the workmen 
will be paid their bonus dues, leave wages, and other legal dues, if not already paid, 
over and above the retrenchment compensation mentioned in annexure ‘A’. 

3 . I find that the terms of the compromise are quite fair. I, therefore, accept it, 

4- Accordingly I dispose of the reference In terms of the compromise which will, 
■along with annexure ‘A’, form part of my award. Let this award be submitted to 
the Central Government under section 16, of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947- 

Sd/- Kamla Sahai, 

Presiding Officer.

Terms of Settlement.

as retrenched under the provisions of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 
on and from the date of termination from their respective services. 

(t>) The workmen concerned shall be paid their dues on above account as

amount shall be paid to them by the Management on or before 24-/-6M. 

(3) That the parties agree to bear their own cost- 

(4) That an Award in terms of the above Settlement may kindly be given. 

It is, therefore, humbly prayed that your Honour may be graciously pleased 
to accept this settlement as fair and reasonable arid your Honour may 
be further pleased to give the Award in terms of this Settlement. 

And for this act of your kindness both the parties shall, as in duty bound, ever 
pray.

(ii) If not, to what relief are the workmen entitled?,” 


2. Both parties have entered Into a compromise. They have filed the com¬ 
promise petition dated the 22nd June, 1968 today. The agreement substantially 
Id this that the orders of transfer passed against the 141 workmen will be treated 
as orders of retrenchment. All these 141 workmen will, therefore, be paid 
retrenchment compensation. This agreement has been signed by the Director 
of the Managing Agents on behalf of the company and by Shrl Lalit Burman, 
General Secretary of the Bihar Koyla Mnjdoor Sabha. on behalf of the union. 


3. It has been orally stated by Shrl Kapoor, the learned Advocate for the 
employers, and by Shri Lalit Burman that the dues mentioned against the names 
of all the 141 workmen are only the amounts payable as. retrenchment compensa¬ 
tion- The workmen are entitled to get their bonus, leave wages and other legal 
dues, If not already paid, over and above the amounts mentiond against the 
names of the workmen in the list attached to the compromise petition-’ 

4. In my opinion, the compromise is fair and reasonable. I accept it. 


5. The reference is, therefore, disposed of in terms of the compromise which, 
along with the list, will form part of the- award. Let this award be submitted 
to the Central Government under section 15 of the Industrial Disputes Act 1947. 


Presiding Officer.

BEFORE THE PRESIDING OFFICER, CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL 

TRIBUNAL NO- 1 DHANBAD, 

In the matter erf Reference No. 68 of 1967 (Old Reference No- 37 of 1967) 

Between 

Employers in relation to Baliharl Colliery of M/s. Baliharl Colliery Co., Pvt. 
Ltd., P.O. Kusunda, Dist. Dhanbad. 

AND 

Their Workmen 
Joint petition of compromise. 

Both the parties in this case respectively submit:— 

( 1 ) That this case is fixed for hearing before your honour- 

(2) That both the parties without prejudice to their respective stands, after 
■mutual negotiations, have amicably resolved this disputes on the following terms:— 

Terms of Settlement 

(a) The Management agrees to treat the workmen concerned in this dispute 

as retrenched under the provisions of the Ir^dustrial Disputes Act, 
1947 on and from the date of their respective transfers. 

(b) The workmen concerned shall be paid their dues on above account as 

shown against each In the Annexure A’ attached herewith and this 
amount shall be paid to them by the Management on or before 24th 
July 1968. 

(3) That the parties agree to bear their own cost. 

(4) That an Award in terms of the above Settlement may kindly be given- 

It Is, therefore, humbly prayed that your Honour may be graciously pleased to 
accept this settlement as fair and reasonable and your Honour may be further 
pleased to give t.hc Award in terms of this Settlement. 

And for this act of your kindness both the parties shall, as in duty bound, 
every pray. 

Dated 22nd June, 1968.

New Delhi , the 2 nd July 1968 

S.O. 2503 .—Whereas the employers in relation to S. C. Rungta Colliery, Post 
Office Rungta Colliery, District Shahdol (Madhya Pradesh) and their workmen 
represented by the Rungta Colliery Mazdoor Sangh, Post Office Rungta Colliery, 
District Shahdol (Madhya Pradesh), have jointly applied to the Central Govern¬ 
ment under sub-section ( 2 ) of section 10 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 
of 1947 ) lor reference to a Tribunal of an Industrial dispute that exists between 
them in respect of the matter set forth in the said application and mentioned in 
the Schedule hereto annexed; 

And whereas the Central Government is satisfied that the persons applying 
represent the majority of each party; 

Now, therefore, In exercise of the powers conferred by sub-section ( 2 ) of 
section 10 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947 ), the Central Govern¬ 
ment hereby refers the said dispute for adjudication to the Central Government 
Industrial Tribunal, Jabalpur, constituted under section 7 A of the said Act. 

Schedule 

In view of the provisions contained in the Payment ol Bonus Act, 1965 (21 of 
19 G 5 ) whether the workers of Messrs Rungta Colliery, Burhar (Madhya Pradesh) 
are entitled for bonus for the years 1966 and 1967 .

S.O. 2506 —In pursuance of section 17 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 
of 1947 ), the Central Government hereby publishes the following award of the 
Central Government Industrial Tribunal, Dhanbad, in the industrial dispute 
between the employers in relation to Messrs Eastern Manganese and Minerals 
Limited, Domchanch, District Hazaribagh and their workmen, which was 
received by the Central Government on the 26 th June, 1968 . 

BEFORE THE CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL-CUM- 
LABOUR COURT AT DHANBAD- 

In the matter of a reference under section 10 ( 1 ) (d) of the Industrial Dis¬ 
putes Act, 1947 . 


Reference No. 107 of 1905 


Parties: 

Employers in relation to Messrs Eastern Manganese and Minerals Limited, 
Domchanch, District Hazaribaeh. 

Vs- 


Their Workmen. 


Present; 

Shri Kamla Sahai, Presiding Offlcer. 

Appeaha nces; 

For the Employers .—Shri Girdhar Gopal. 

For the Workmen -—Shri B. D. Bansal and Shri Jagar Nath Singh. 

State: Bihar. Industry: Mica. 

Camp: Patna, dated the 18 th June, 1968 . 

AWARD 

The employers in relation to Messrs Eastern Manganese and Minerals Limited 
Sibsagar, passed orders of transfer of throe workmen namely Shri .Tiwat Sineh" 
Darwan, from Domchanch Mica Mine to the SIbsagar factory owned by M/s’ 
Chrestion Mica Industries Limited, P. O. Domchanch; Sri B. D- Bansal, "Checker 
from Bhelwarla No- 12 Mica Mine to Sibsngar Factory owned by M/s, Chrestien 



356 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JULY 13, 1968/ASADHA 22, 1890 [1'art II- 


lica Industries Limited, P.O. Domchanch, and Shri Jagan Nath Singh, Pump - 
Jialasi, from Kukurkolwa Mica Mine to the Sibsagar Workshop owned by M/s. 
hrestien Mica Industries Limited, P, O. Domchanch. 

2 . The employers’ case is that they have the right to make the transfers and 
lat the workmen did in fact join their duties at the places of transfer but the 
ibrakh Mazdoor Union raised a dispute through its General Secretary, Harish 
handra Mlshra. Conciliation proceedings failed and the Central Government 
lade a reference under section 10 ( 1 ) (d) of the Industrial Disputes Act to the 
-entral Government Industrial Tribunal at Dhanbad. The first question put in 
' e schedule was whether the transfers of the three workmen as mentioned above 
’■ere acts of victimisation of the workmen concerned. The second question which 
las put in the schedule was that, if the transfers were acts of victimisation; what 
elief were the workmen entitled to? 

3 - It seems that the Abrakh Mazdor Union entered into an agreement with 
le management of the employers- The agreement was signed by the General 
Manager of the employers on behalf of the management, by Shri H. C. Mlshra, 
feneral Secretary of the union on behalf of the union and also by the Asstt. 
,abour Commissioner, Hazaribagh, on the 22 nd February I 960 - It was filed bo¬ 
or the Tribunal on the 26 th February, I 960 - 

4 . One of the workmen namely Shri B. D- Bansal filed an application before 
' Tribunal on his own behalf as well as on behalf of Shri Jagan Nath Singh that 
ney did not know anything about the terms of the settlement and that they were 
lot prepared to agree to the settlement. He prayed that the reference be heard 
m merits. The other workman namely Jiwat Singh did not appear and it has 
ieen stated in the agreement that the union did" hot press the ease of Jiwat Singh 
,s he had already taken whatever was due to him on full and final settlement. 

5 . The then Presiding Officer, Shri Raj Kishore Prasad, heard the parties and, 
vhlle giving the award in terms of the settlement in so far as Jiwat Singh was 
:oncerned, he did not accept the settlement in so far as B. D. Bansal and Jagan 
lath Singh were concerned- He gave an award In accordance with his judgment 
s to the merits of the case- 

6 . The management then filed C.W.J.C- No. 308 of 1967 in the High Court, 
’his case has been disposed of by their Lordships the Chief Justice and Mr. Justice 
I N- Jha by a judgment dated the 8th April, 1968 . Their Lordship have, in the 

_t. paragraph of their judgment, said as follows:— 

“For the reasons stated above, the application is allowed and the award of 
the Industrial Tribunal (Central), Dhanbad dated the 28 th March, 1967 
Is quashed and the Tribunal Is directed to make an award in terms 
of the settlement dated February 22, 1966 , filed before it on the 26 th 
February, 1966 (Annexure ‘B’). The petitioners are entitled to costs- 
, Hearing fee: Es. 100/-”, 

7 . I issued notices upon all the parties. The management has appeared today 
hrough Shri Giridhar Gopal. The union has not appeared at all. The workman, 
jhri B. D. Bansal and Shri Jagan Nath Singh have appeared in person. The ori¬ 
ginal agreement signed by the parties is already on the record as annexure ‘A’. 
Jhe workmen have not given anv reason why an award should not be passed In 
erms of the direction given by Their Lordship of the Patna High Court. I have 
;onsldered the terms of the award and it seems to be that they are fair and reason¬ 
able. I, therefore, accept the agreement and direct that this reference should be 

Isposed of in terms of the agreement dated the 22 nd February, 1060 which was 
” d before this Tribunal op the 26 th February, 1966 , That will form part of my 
twarcl. This is my aw*ard which may be submitted to the Central Government! 

nder section 15 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 .

Short Recital 

This Union has raised a dispute regarding the transfer of Shri Jiwat Singh, 
Durwan, Shri B, D. Bonsai, Checker and Shri Jagannath Singh, Pump Khalas 
This dispute has been referred to adjudication by the Government of India, Minis 
try of Labour and Employment vide their order dated 18 th June 1965 - 

The Union has also raised a General Demand regarding the right of the 
Management for transferring the employees from one place to another or from 
one sister concern to another, 

After the reference the parties showed a desire to discuss this dispute mutually 
As a result of the mutual discussions held on 22 nd February, 1966 in the presence 
of the Assistant Labour Commissioner (Central), Hazaribagh, they have arrivec 
at Ihe following settlement- 


Terms of Settlement 


( 1 ) It is agreed by both the parties that Shri B. D. Bansal will be paid Rs 
705 / 54 p (Rupees seven hundred and five and paise fifty four only) in full and ftna 
settlement of all his dues on this Management, within a week from the date of thi 
settlement. After the acceptance of this amount Shri Bansal will have no clahr 
whatsoever over this Management. This payment is over and above his leavi 
wages and earned wages. 


( 2 ) Shri Jagannath Singh will be given one additional increment with effec 
from 1 st February, 1966 - He will have no claim for his transfer and the Unioi 
will also drop this matter before the Hon’ble Tribunal. 

( 3 ) The Union is not pressing the case of Shri Jiwat Singh, Durwan as h 
has already taken in full and final settlement. 

( 4 ) The Union recognises the right of the Management to transfer the monthly 
paid employees from one place to another or from one concern to another slstei 
concern and in future they will not raise any demand or dispute in this respect. 

( 5 ) The Union is agreeable to send one copy of this agreement to the Honour 
able Tribunal with the request that an award on the lines may please be given. 

Representing the employers: 


For Eastern Manganese and Minerals Ltd 
Sd./- R. G. Agarwala, 

General Manager

For ABRAKH MAZDOOR UNION
